		</li>
		<li class="gallerybox" style="width: 155px">
			<div class="thumb" style="width: 150px; height: 150px;"><span typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/File:Buddhist_alms_in_Si_Phan_Don.jpg" class="mw-file-description" title="Buddhist monks in Laos collecting alms"><img alt="Buddhist monks in Laos collecting alms" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/12/Buddhist_alms_in_Si_Phan_Don.jpg/120px-Buddhist_alms_in_Si_Phan_Don.jpg" decoding="async" width="120" height="80" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/12/Buddhist_alms_in_Si_Phan_Don.jpg/250px-Buddhist_alms_in_Si_Phan_Don.jpg 1.5x" data-file-width="5966" data-file-height="3977" /></a></span></div>
			<div class="gallerytext"><a href="/wiki/Bhikkhu" title="Bhikkhu">Buddhist monks</a> in Laos collecting <a href="/wiki/Pindapata" title="Pindapata">alms</a></div>
		</li>
</ul>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Culture">Culture</h2></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1320445320" /><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Culture_of_Asia" title="Culture of Asia">Culture of Asia</a></div>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/Culture_of_Asia" title="Culture of Asia">culture of Asia</a> is a diverse blend of customs and traditions that have been practiced by the various ethnic groups of the continent for centuries. The continent is divided into six geographic sub-regions: <a href="/wiki/Central_Asia" title="Central Asia">Central Asia</a>, <a href="/wiki/East_Asian_cultural_sphere" class="mw-redirect" title="East Asian cultural sphere">East Asia</a>, <a href="/wiki/North_Asia" title="North Asia">North Asia</a>, <a href="/wiki/South_Asian_ethnic_groups" class="mw-redirect" title="South Asian ethnic groups">South Asia</a>, <a href="/wiki/Southeast_Asia" title="Southeast Asia">Southeast Asia</a>, and <a href="/wiki/West_Asia" title="West Asia">West Asia</a>.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-180" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-180"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>167<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> These regions are defined by their cultural similarities, including common religions, languages, and ethnicities. West Asia, also known as Southwest Asia or the <a href="/wiki/Middle_East" title="Middle East">Middle East</a>, has cultural roots in the ancient civilisations of the <a href="/wiki/Fertile_Crescent" title="Fertile Crescent">Fertile Crescent</a> and <a href="/wiki/Mesopotamia" title="Mesopotamia">Mesopotamia</a>, which gave rise to the <a href="/wiki/Iran" title="Iran">Persian</a>, <a href="/wiki/Arab_world" title="Arab world">Arab</a>, <a href="/wiki/Ottoman_Empire" title="Ottoman Empire">Ottoman</a> empires, as well as the Abrahamic religions of Judaism, Christianity and Islam.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-181" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-181"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>168<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> These civilisations, which are located in the <a href="/wiki/Hilly_flanks" class="mw-redirect" title="Hilly flanks">Hilly flanks</a>, are among the oldest in the world, with evidence of farming dating back to around 9000&#160;BCE.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-182" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-182"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>169<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> Despite the challenges posed by the vast size of the continent and the presence of natural barriers such as deserts and mountain ranges, trade and commerce have helped to create a <a href="/wiki/Pan-Asian" class="mw-redirect" title="Pan-Asian">Pan-Asian</a> culture that is shared across the region.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-183" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-183"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>170<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Nobel_laureates">Nobel laureates</h3></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Rabindranath_Tagore_in_1909.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/91/Rabindranath_Tagore_in_1909.jpg/250px-Rabindranath_Tagore_in_1909.jpg" decoding="async" width="200" height="280" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/91/Rabindranath_Tagore_in_1909.jpg/330px-Rabindranath_Tagore_in_1909.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/91/Rabindranath_Tagore_in_1909.jpg/500px-Rabindranath_Tagore_in_1909.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1071" data-file-height="1500" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/Rabindranath_Tagore" title="Rabindranath Tagore">Rabindranath Tagore</a>, winner of the <a href="/wiki/Nobel_Prize_for_Literature" class="mw-redirect" title="Nobel Prize for Literature">Nobel Prize for Literature</a> in 1913, and Asia's first Nobel laureate</figcaption></figure>
<p><a href="/wiki/Rabindranath_Tagore" title="Rabindranath Tagore">Rabindranath Tagore</a>, a <a href="/wiki/Bengali_literature" title="Bengali literature">Bengali</a> dramatist and author from <a href="/wiki/Santiniketan" class="mw-redirect" title="Santiniketan">Santiniketan</a> (now in <a href="/wiki/West_Bengal" title="West Bengal">West Bengal</a>, India), won the <a href="/wiki/Nobel_Prize_in_Literature" title="Nobel Prize in Literature">Nobel Prize in Literature</a> in 1913, becoming the first Asian <a href="/wiki/Nobel_laureate" class="mw-redirect" title="Nobel laureate">Nobel laureate</a>. The prize was awarded for Tagore's  prose works and poetry, which had a significant additional impact on national literatures throughout the Western world. Tagore also authored both the Indian and Bangladeshi national anthems.
</p><p>Other Asian writers who won Nobel Prize for literature include <a href="/wiki/Yasunari_Kawabata" title="Yasunari Kawabata">Yasunari Kawabata</a> (Japan, 1968), <a href="/wiki/Kenzabur%C5%8D_%C5%8Ce" title="Kenzaburō Ōe">Kenzaburō Ōe</a> (Japan, 1994), <a href="/wiki/Gao_Xingjian" title="Gao Xingjian">Gao Xingjian</a> (China, 2000), <a href="/wiki/Orhan_Pamuk" title="Orhan Pamuk">Orhan Pamuk</a> (Turkey, 2006), <a href="/wiki/Mo_Yan" title="Mo Yan">Mo Yan</a> (China, 2012), and <a href="/wiki/Han_Kang" title="Han Kang">Han Kang</a> (South Korea, 2024). Some may consider the American writer, <a href="/wiki/Pearl_S._Buck" title="Pearl S. Buck">Pearl S. Buck</a>, an honorary Asian Nobel laureate, having spent considerable time in China as the daughter of missionaries, and based many of her novels, namely <i><a href="/wiki/The_Good_Earth" title="The Good Earth">The Good Earth</a></i> (1931) and <i><a href="/wiki/The_Mother_(1934_novel)" class="mw-redirect" title="The Mother (1934 novel)">The Mother</a></i> (1933), as well as the biographies of her parents for their time in China, <i><a href="/wiki/The_Exile_(1936_book)" class="mw-redirect" title="The Exile (1936 book)">The Exile</a></i> and <i><a href="/wiki/Fighting_Angel" title="Fighting Angel">Fighting Angel</a></i>, all of which earned her the Literature prize in 1938.
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Mother_Teresa" title="Mother Teresa">Mother Teresa</a> of India and <a href="/wiki/Shirin_Ebadi" title="Shirin Ebadi">Shirin Ebadi</a> of Iran were awarded the Nobel Peace Prize for their significant and pioneering efforts for <a href="/wiki/Democracy" title="Democracy">democracy</a> and human rights, especially for the rights of women and children. Ebadi is the first Iranian and the first Muslim woman to receive the prize. Another Nobel Peace Prize winner is <a href="/wiki/Aung_San_Suu_Kyi" title="Aung San Suu Kyi">Aung San Suu Kyi</a> from <a href="/wiki/Burma" class="mw-redirect" title="Burma">Burma</a> for her peaceful and non-violent struggle under a military dictatorship in Burma. She is a nonviolent pro-democracy activist and leader of the National League for Democracy in Burma (Myanmar) and a noted prisoner of conscience. She is a <a href="/wiki/Buddhist" class="mw-redirect" title="Buddhist">Buddhist</a> and was awarded the Nobel Peace Prize in 1991. Chinese dissident <a href="/wiki/Liu_Xiaobo" title="Liu Xiaobo">Liu Xiaobo</a> was awarded the Nobel Peace Prize for "his long and non-violent struggle for fundamental human rights in China" on 8 October 2010. He is the first Chinese citizen to be awarded a Nobel Prize of any kind while residing in China. In 2014, <a href="/wiki/Kailash_Satyarthi" title="Kailash Satyarthi">Kailash Satyarthi</a> from India and <a href="/wiki/Malala_Yousafzai" title="Malala Yousafzai">Malala Yousafzai</a> from Pakistan were awarded the Nobel Peace Prize "for their struggle against the suppression of children and young people and for the right of all children to education".
</p><p><a href="/wiki/C.V._Raman" class="mw-redirect" title="C.V. Raman">C.V. Raman</a> is the first Asian to get a Nobel prize in Sciences. He won the <a href="/wiki/Nobel_Prize_in_Physics" title="Nobel Prize in Physics">Nobel Prize in Physics</a> "for his work on the scattering of light and for the discovery of the <a href="/wiki/Raman_scattering" title="Raman scattering">effect named after him</a>".
</p><p>Japan has won the most Nobel Prizes of any Asian nation with 24 followed by India which has won 13.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="margin-left:0.1em; white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Manual_of_Style/Dates_and_numbers#Chronological_items" title="Wikipedia:Manual of Style/Dates and numbers"><span title="The text near this tag is dated. (January 2025)">needs update</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Amartya_Sen" title="Amartya Sen">Amartya Sen</a> (<abbr title="born" style="white-space:nowrap">b.</abbr>&#8201;1933) is an Indian economist who was awarded the 1998 Nobel Memorial Prize in Economic Sciences for his contributions to welfare economics and <a href="/wiki/Social_choice_theory" title="Social choice theory">social choice theory</a>, and for his interest in the problems of society's poorest members.
</p><p>Other Asian Nobel Prize winners include <a href="/wiki/Subrahmanyan_Chandrasekhar" title="Subrahmanyan Chandrasekhar">Subrahmanyan Chandrasekhar</a>, <a href="/wiki/Abdus_Salam" title="Abdus Salam">Abdus Salam</a>, <a href="/wiki/Robert_Aumann" title="Robert Aumann">Robert Aumann</a>, <a href="/wiki/Menachem_Begin" title="Menachem Begin">Menachem Begin</a>, <a href="/wiki/Aaron_Ciechanover" title="Aaron Ciechanover">Aaron Ciechanover</a>, <a href="/wiki/Avram_Hershko" title="Avram Hershko">Avram Hershko</a>, <a href="/wiki/Daniel_Kahneman" title="Daniel Kahneman">Daniel Kahneman</a>, <a href="/wiki/Shimon_Peres" title="Shimon Peres">Shimon Peres</a>, <a href="/wiki/Yitzhak_Rabin" title="Yitzhak Rabin">Yitzhak Rabin</a>, <a href="/wiki/Ada_Yonath" title="Ada Yonath">Ada Yonath</a>, <a href="/wiki/Yasser_Arafat" title="Yasser Arafat">Yasser Arafat</a>, <a href="/wiki/Jos%C3%A9_Ramos-Horta" title="José Ramos-Horta">José Ramos-Horta</a> and Bishop <a href="/wiki/Carlos_Filipe_Ximenes_Belo" title="Carlos Filipe Ximenes Belo">Carlos Filipe Ximenes Belo</a> of <a href="/wiki/Timor-Leste" title="Timor-Leste">Timor-Leste</a>, <a href="/wiki/Kim_Dae-jung" title="Kim Dae-jung">Kim Dae-jung</a>, and 13 Japanese scientists. Most of the said awardees are from Japan and <a href="/wiki/Israel" title="Israel">Israel</a> except for Chandrasekhar and Raman (India), Abdus Salam (Pakistan), Arafat (Palestinian Territories), Kim (South Korea), and Horta and Belo (Timor-Leste).<sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="margin-left:0.1em; white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Manual_of_Style/Dates_and_numbers#Chronological_items" title="Wikipedia:Manual of Style/Dates and numbers"><span title="The text near this tag is dated. (January 2025)">needs update</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>In 2006, the Bangladeshi <a href="/wiki/Muhammad_Yunus" title="Muhammad Yunus">Muhammad Yunus</a> of  was awarded the Nobel Peace Prize for the establishment of <a href="/wiki/Grameen_Bank" title="Grameen Bank">Grameen Bank</a>, a community development bank that lends money to poor people, especially women. He is known for the concept of micro credit which, allows poor and destitute people to borrow money. The borrowers pay back money within the specified period and defaulting is very low. Yunus also became the leader of an <a href="/wiki/Yunus_interim_government" class="mw-redirect" title="Yunus interim government">interim government</a> after the <a href="/wiki/2024_Bangladesh_quota_reform_movement" title="2024 Bangladesh quota reform movement">2024 Bangladesh quota reform movement</a>.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-184" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-184"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>171<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Dalai_Lama" title="Dalai Lama">Dalai Lama</a> received the Nobel Peace Prize, in <a href="/wiki/Oslo" title="Oslo">Oslo</a>, Norway in 1989.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-185" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-185"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>172<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p>

<div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Notes">Notes</h2></div>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1327269900">.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns-2{column-width:30em}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns-3{column-width:25em}body.skin-vector-2022 .mw-parser-output .reflist-columns-2{column-width:27em}body.skin-vector-2022 .mw-parser-output .reflist-columns-3{column-width:22.5em}.mw-parser-output .references[data-mw-group=upper-alpha]{list-style-type:upper-alpha}.mw-parser-output .references[data-mw-group=upper-roman]{list-style-type:upper-roman}.mw-parser-output .references[data-mw-group=lower-alpha]{list-style-type:lower-alpha}.mw-parser-output .references[data-mw-group=lower-greek]{list-style-type:lower-greek}.mw-parser-output .references[data-mw-group=lower-roman]{list-style-type:lower-roman}.mw-parser-output div.reflist-liststyle-upper-alpha .references{list-style-type:upper-alpha}.mw-parser-output div.reflist-liststyle-upper-roman .references{list-style-type:upper-roman}.mw-parser-output div.reflist-liststyle-lower-alpha .references{list-style-type:lower-alpha}.mw-parser-output div.reflist-liststyle-lower-greek .references{list-style-type:lower-greek}.mw-parser-output div.reflist-liststyle-lower-roman .references{list-style-type:lower-roman}</style><div class="mw-references-wrap mw-references-columns" style="column-width: calc( 0.9 * 30em );"></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1327269900" /><div class="mw-references-wrap mw-references-columns" style="column-width: calc( 0.9 * 30em );"><ol class="references" data-mw-group="note">
<li id="cite&#95;note-10"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-10">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">Asia is normally considered its own continent in the English speaking world, which uses the seven continent model.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-8" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-8"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>8<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup><sup id="cite&#95;ref-9" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-9"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>9<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> Other models consider Asia as part of a Eurasian or Afro-Eurasian continent (see <a href="/wiki/Continent#Number" title="Continent">Continent §&#160;Number</a>).</span>
</li>
<li id="cite&#95;note-13"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-13">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">44,579,000 square kilometres (17,212,000&#160;square&#160;miles)</span>
</li>
<li id="cite&#95;note-95"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-95">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">Siberia lies in Asia geographically, but is considered part of Europe culturally and politically.</span>
</li>
<li id="cite&#95;note-104"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-104">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">Azerbaijan is a <a href="/wiki/Transcontinental_country" class="mw-redirect" title="Transcontinental country">transcontinental country</a>, straddling the border between <a href="/wiki/Europe" title="Europe">Europe</a> and Asia. It's culture is influenced by both <a href="/wiki/Culture_of_Europe" title="Culture of Europe">European</a> and <a href="/wiki/Culture_of_Asia" title="Culture of Asia">Asian</a> customs.</span>
</li>
<li id="cite&#95;note-105"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-105">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">Egypt is a <a href="/wiki/Transcontinental_country" class="mw-redirect" title="Transcontinental country">transcontinental country</a> located primarily in <a href="/wiki/Africa" title="Africa">Africa</a>, with the <a href="/wiki/Sinai_Peninsula" title="Sinai Peninsula">Sinai Peninsula</a> being located in Asia.</span>
</li>
<li id="cite&#95;note-106"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-106">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">Cairo is located in <a href="/wiki/Africa" title="Africa">Africa</a>.</span>
</li>
<li id="cite&#95;note-107"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-107">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">Georgia is a <a href="/wiki/Transcontinental_country" class="mw-redirect" title="Transcontinental country">transcontinental country</a>, with a small northern portion lying in <a href="/wiki/Europe" title="Europe">Europe</a>. Despite being primarily located in Asia, Georgia leans strongly towards <a href="/wiki/Culture_of_Europe" title="Culture of Europe">European culture</a> and has closer ties with the <a href="/wiki/United_Nations" title="United Nations">UN</a> and <a href="/wiki/NATO" title="NATO">NATO</a>.</span>
</li>
<li id="cite&#95;note-108"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-108">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">Indonesia is a <a href="/wiki/Transcontinental_country" class="mw-redirect" title="Transcontinental country">transcontinental country</a>, with the island of <a href="/wiki/New_Guinea" title="New Guinea">New Guinea</a> being located in <a href="/wiki/Melanesia" title="Melanesia">Melanesia</a> - a region of <a href="/wiki/Oceania" title="Oceania">Oceania</a>. New Guinea's eastern portion is shared with <a href="/wiki/Papua_New_Guinea" title="Papua New Guinea">Papua New Guinea</a>.</span>
</li>
<li id="cite&#95;note-109"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-109">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">Kazakhstan is a <a href="/wiki/Transcontinental_country" class="mw-redirect" title="Transcontinental country">transcontinental country</a>, with about 15% of its land located in <a href="/wiki/Europe" title="Europe">Europe</a>.</span>
</li>
<li id="cite&#95;note-110"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-110">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">Russia is a <a href="/wiki/Transcontinental_country" class="mw-redirect" title="Transcontinental country">transcontinental country</a> located in <a href="/wiki/Eastern_Europe" title="Eastern Europe">Eastern Europe</a> and <a href="/wiki/North_Asia" title="North Asia">North Asia</a>, but is considered European historically, culturally, ethnically, and politically, and the vast majority of its population (78%) lives within its <a href="/wiki/European_Russia" title="European Russia">European part</a>.</span>
</li>
<li id="cite&#95;note-111"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-111">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">Moscow is located in <a href="/wiki/Europe" title="Europe">Europe</a>.</span>
</li>
<li id="cite&#95;note-112"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-112">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">Turkey is a <a href="/wiki/Transcontinental_country" class="mw-redirect" title="Transcontinental country">transcontinental country</a> located mainly in <a href="/wiki/West_Asia" title="West Asia">West Asia</a> with a smaller portion in <a href="/wiki/Southeastern_Europe" class="mw-redirect" title="Southeastern Europe">Southeastern Europe</a>.</span>
</li>
<li id="cite&#95;note-113"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-113">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">Yemen is a <a href="/wiki/Transcontinental_country" class="mw-redirect" title="Transcontinental country">transcontinental country</a>. While its mainland is geographically part of Asia, the <a href="/wiki/Socotra_Archipelago" class="mw-redirect" title="Socotra Archipelago">Socotra Archipelago</a> islands are geographically located in <a href="/wiki/Africa" title="Africa">Africa</a>.</span>
</li>
</ol></div>
